--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
I'll analyze the aggregation mission carefully.

## Reasoning

**Mission analysis:** Score increases by 1 for every robot outside the target region (BLACK floor, BLUE walls) at every step. So robots need to find the BLACK strip with BLUE walls and stay there.

**Key perceptions available:**
- Floor color: BLACK (target strip and other strip), GRAY (middle area)
- Wall colors: BLUE (target) and GREEN (other strip) visible via camera
- Neighbor counts

**Strategy:**
- BLACK floor = could be either strip. Need to distinguish: BLUE walls = target, GREEN walls = wrong strip
- When on BLACK floor with BLUE walls visible → STAY (Stop or slow movement)
- When on GRAY or BLACK with GREEN walls → move toward BLUE walls
- GoToColor with color BLUE (clr=3? Let me check: colors are {1,2,3,4,5,6}) - need to figure out color mapping. Blue walls = color 3 likely, cyan=6, etc. Since walls are BLUE, I'll try clr=3 for blue.

**State designs:**
- State 0: Explore/GoToColor(blue) to find BLUE walls
- State 1: Stop when on BLACK floor and BLUE walls perceived
- State 2: GoAwayColor(green) to escape green strip

Let me try several topologies:

**Machine 1 (elitism baseline):** 3 states: Explore → if BLACK floor → check for blue walls → Stop
**Machine 2:** 2 states: GoToColor(blue) + Stop on black floor
**Machine 3:** 3 states with GoAwayColor(green) + GoToColor(blue) + Stop

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
I need to see the results from Round 2 to know which machines performed best. Since no scores were provided, I'll reason from first principles and the mission structure, then design machines that should work well.

## Reasoning

**Mission recap:** Robots need to reach the BLACK strip bordered by BLUE walls. The other BLACK strip has GREEN walls. Both strips have BLACK floors; the middle is GRAY.

**Key insight:** 
- GoToColor(blue/clr=3) should guide robots toward BLUE walls = target strip
- GoAwayColor(green/clr=2) should push robots away from wrong strip
- BLACK floor = arrived at a strip (could be either)
- Once on BLACK with BLUE walls visible → Stop
- Once on BLACK with GREEN walls visible → escape (GoToColor blue or GoAwayColor green)

**Refined strategies:**
- The most reliable signal: if robot sees BLUE walls (C=7, l=3) it's near target → approach
- If robot sees GREEN walls (C=7, l=2) it's near wrong strip → flee
- On BLACK floor without BLUE walls → explore to find them
- On BLACK floor with BLUE walls → stop

I'll concentrate on GoToColor(blue) as primary behavior with Stop on black+blue-wall detection, and GoAwayColor(green) as escape from wrong strip.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
Since I'm not receiving score feedback between rounds, I need to reason more carefully about what should work best for this mission.

## Reasoning

**Core problem:** Robots start in the middle (GRAY floor). They need to reach the BLACK strip with BLUE walls and stay there. The challenge is distinguishing the two BLACK strips.

**Critical insight I may have missed:** When a robot is on BLACK floor and sees BLUE walls → it's in the TARGET zone → Stop. When it sees GREEN walls on BLACK → wrong strip → escape. But GoToColor(blue=3) moves toward BLUE walls, which should pull robots toward the target strip from anywhere in the arena.

**Most reliable FSM logic:**
- State 0: GoToColor(BLUE walls) - actively seeks the target
- State 1: Stop - when on BLACK floor (arrived at a strip)
- Transition: BLACK floor → Stop; if then GREEN walls visible → go back to GoToColor(blue)

**The problem with previous designs:** Stopping on ANY black floor (including wrong strip). Need to only stop when BLUE walls are visible AND on black floor.

**Better design:**
- State 0: GoToColor(blue) 
- State 1: Stop, but if GREEN walls seen → go to GoAwayColor(green) or back to GoToColor(blue)
- Transition to Stop: only when BLACK floor AND NOT seeing green (hard to combine) OR when seeing BLUE walls

Let me try: Stop only triggered by seeing BLUE walls (C=7, l=3) while on black, not by black floor alone.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
Since I'm still not receiving score feedback, I need to think more carefully about what might be failing and try more diverse approaches.

## Reasoning

**Fundamental re-analysis:**
- Robots start in GRAY (middle). Score = robots outside target BLACK+BLUE strip.
- GoToColor(blue, clr=3) moves toward BLUE walls = correct direction
- But: once a robot enters the target strip, it's on BLACK floor with BLUE walls. If it keeps moving it might leave. So Stop is important once there.
- The transition "on BLACK floor → Stop" is risky because both strips are BLACK.
- Better: "sees BLUE walls → Stop" since BLUE walls only visible near target strip.

**New key insight:** Maybe the color IDs aren't what I think. Let me reconsider: colors 1-6 could be red, green, blue, yellow, magenta, cyan. So blue=3, green=2. But I should also try other color IDs to hedge.

**Also:** Maybe the issue is that GoToColor moves toward the color but overshoots. A combination of exploration + attraction to blue walls + stop when on black near blue walls could be more robust.

**Most promising architecture:** 3 states:
- S0: GoToColor(blue) - seek blue walls
- S1: Stop - when blue walls visible (stay in target zone)  
- S2: GoAwayColor(green) - when on black with green walls, escape

With S1→S0 if gray floor (left the zone), S1→S2 if green walls appear.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
Since I'm still not receiving score feedback, I need to fundamentally reconsider my approach. Let me think more carefully.

## Reasoning

**What might be wrong with my designs:**

1. **Color ID mapping uncertainty:** I've been assuming blue=3, green=2. But the color mapping in AutoMoDe might be: 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan. This seems right but let me hedge by trying other IDs.

2. **GoToColor behavior:** It moves toward colored objects detected by camera. Wall colors visible from inside the strip and approaching it. But maybe from the center, neither wall is visible, so GoToColor does nothing useful until close.

3. **The AGGREGATION label:** Wait - this says "AGGREGATION mission" but the scoring description says "score increases by 1 for every robot outside the target region." That's actually a **homing/foraging** mission, not pure aggregation. Maybe I'm overcomplicating this.

4. **Simple approach:** Just GoToColor(blue walls) + Stop when on black. The black floor detection is simpler and more reliable than wall color detection from a distance. The risk of stopping on the wrong black strip is real but maybe the blue wall detection from Stop state handles escape.

5. **Try purely exploration-based:** Random walk until black floor, then stop. Simple but robots might end up in wrong strip.

**New strategy - try very simple 1-state machines and also try clr values 4,5,6:**

FSM: --nstates 1 --s0 8 --vel0 1.0 --clr0 3 --cle0 0 --n0 0

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
Since I'm receiving no score feedback, I need to fundamentally rethink my approach. Let me reconsider from scratch.

## Reasoning

**Critical re-examination:**

The problem says "AGGREGATION mission" but scores by robots outside a target region. This is actually a **homing** task. The target is the BLACK strip with BLUE walls.

**What I might be getting wrong:**

1. **Color IDs for walls:** The problem says walls are BLUE or GREEN. In AutoMoDe, the camera detects colored objects. The color IDs are robot LED colors: 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan. But WALL colors might be different from LED colors - the camera condition C=7 detects colored objects which could be walls OR other robots' LEDs. If walls are painted blue/green, they should appear as blue(3)/green(2).

2. **Key problem:** Maybe GoToColor for wall colors doesn't work as expected because walls are large and the robot always "sees" them from anywhere. If the robot always sees both blue and green walls, GoToColor might not differentiate well.

3. **Floor-based approach might be more reliable:** GRAY = middle (not target), BLACK = one of two strips. The problem: can't distinguish black strips by floor alone.

4. **Hybrid:** Explore until black floor, then check wall color to confirm target. If green walls seen → escape back to gray.

5. **Maybe the simplest thing works:** GoToColor(blue) continuously without stopping, since robots that reach blue walls will hover near them. Or exploration until black, stop, then flee if green.

**Let me try radically different approaches including single-state and floor-only logic:**

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
Since I'm receiving no score feedback across 7 rounds, I need to completely rethink. Let me be much more systematic.

## Reasoning

**The fundamental problem I may have:** I don't know which machines are scoring well. Without feedback, I must diversify maximally and try genuinely different approaches.

**What I know for certain:**
- Target = BLACK floor + BLUE walls border
- Wrong strip = BLACK floor + GREEN walls border  
- Middle = GRAY floor
- Need robots to gather in target strip and STAY there

**Approaches I haven't tried enough:**
1. **Pure floor-following:** Explore until black, then stop. Half will be in wrong strip but that's 50% efficiency. Maybe acceptable?
2. **Attraction to other robots:** If robots aggregate together AND some find the target, attraction pulls others there too.
3. **Very simple 1-2 state machines** - complexity might be hurting.
4. **FlockCenter/Attraction** - once some robots are in target, others follow.
5. **Different probability values** - maybe my probabilities are too high causing too-eager transitions.

**Key realization:** Maybe the camera CANNOT see wall colors well and C=7 conditions on wall colors never fire, making those transitions dead. If so, my machines are effectively just GoToColor(3) forever or GoToColor then Stop on black floor.

Let me try: floor-only logic, neighbor-based logic, and pure movement behaviors.

# ROUND 9/10

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
Since I've received no score feedback across 8 rounds, I need to make a drastic change in strategy. Let me think very carefully about what's fundamentally right and wrong.

## Reasoning

**Deep re-analysis of the mission:**

The score = sum over all steps of robots outside target. To minimize: get ALL robots into the target BLACK+BLUE strip as fast as possible and keep them there.

**What I've been assuming vs. what might be true:**
- I assume blue walls = color 3, green walls = color 2. This might be correct.
- But maybe the C=7 (perceive color) condition fires based on robot LED colors, NOT wall colors. If walls aren't lit as LEDs, C=7 might never fire for walls at all!
- If C=7 only detects other robots' LEDs, then all my wall-detection transitions are dead code.

**If C=7 only detects robot LEDs:** Then the only useful perceptions are floor color (C=0 black, C=1 gray) and neighbor counts (C=3, C=4).

**Floor-only strategy:**
- GRAY floor = middle, not target → keep moving
- BLACK floor = could be target or wrong strip → 50/50 chance

**But wait:** GoToColor behavior (B=8) uses the camera to move toward colored objects. If walls ARE colored objects detectable by camera, GoToColor(blue) would work. The question is whether it does.

**Most reliable simple strategy:**
- Explore until BLACK floor → Stop. This gets ~50% of robots to target immediately.
- But robots in wrong strip score badly. Need escape mechanism.

**Without reliable wall detection:** Use probabilistic escape - occasionally re-explore from black.

**New approach - try neighbor-based:** If many robots are in target strip together, high neighbor count = good signal. But this requires critical mass.

**Let me try the most diverse set possible including very simple machines:**

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
Since this is the final round and I've received no score feedback across 9 rounds, I need to make my best possible set of machines. Let me think very carefully.

## Reasoning

**Final analysis - what I know:**
- No feedback means I cannot do proper elitism or exploitation. I must cover the space well.
- The mission: robots must be in BLACK strip with BLUE walls. Score = robots outside × steps.
- The two most reliable strategies are floor-based (black→stop) and color-seeking (GoToColor blue).

**Critical insight I may have been missing:** The C=7 condition detects colored objects seen by the camera. In ARGoS, the range-and-bearing doesn't detect walls - the camera (omnidirectional) detects colored blobs. Wall colors ARE detected by the camera as colored blobs. So GoToColor(3=blue) should move toward blue walls.

**However:** GoToColor moves toward the NEAREST blue object. If robots have blue LEDs on, they'd also count. I've been setting LED colors to 6 (cyan) and 0 (off), so blue LED robots won't interfere.

**Best strategy for final round:**
1. Keep the most theoretically sound designs
2. Try floor-only (simple but works for 50%)
3. Try GoToColor(blue) + stop on black floor (might get >50%)
4. Try with escape from green
5. Try pure exploration variants
6. Include neighbor-based designs in case robots cluster

**The simplest correct machine:** GoToColor(blue), stop when on black. If robots overshoot they end up on gray and transition back. Key: don't stop on black with green walls.

################################################################################
# PROMPT SYSTEME
################################################################################

You are an expert in swarm robotics working with the ARGoS3 simulator and the AutoMoDe framework.
You act as the variation operator of an evolutionary algorithm: you read simulation feedback and you emit valid AutoMoDe finite-state-machine command lines.

# 1. THE MISSION
The score is MINIMISED.

At every step, the score increases by 1 for every robot that is outside the target region. The score does not depend on how close the robots are to each other.

The arena has two strips at opposite ends. The floor is BLACK in both strips and GRAY in between. The target region is the strip whose bordering walls are BLUE; the walls bordering the other strip are GREEN.

# 2. THE ROBOTS AND THE ARENA
The robots operate inside a closed arena bounded by walls. 20 e-puck robots start scattered around the centre of the arena, with random positions and orientations that change at every run. Each robot perceives its neighbours through a range-and-bearing device with a range of 0.50 m: neighbour-count conditions only count robots closer than that. A run lasts 1200 simulation steps (120 s at 10 steps per second).

# 3. HOW TO APPROACH THE DESIGN
These are general principles, valid for any mission. They are not a solution to this one.
- Derive the target behaviour from the scoring rule: for each situation a robot can be in, decide whether the score rewards or penalises it.
- Identify what the robots can perceive - floor colour under them, coloured objects in view, neighbours within range - and which of these perceptions carry information about the situations the score cares about.
- Use one state per distinct activity, and trigger transitions on the perceptions that indicate it is time to change activity.

# 4. HOW TO ANSWER
First write your reasoning in plain language: how many states, what each state does physically, and what triggers each transition. Two or three sentences per machine is enough.
Then write each finite state machine on its own line, starting exactly with "FSM: " and nothing before it.

# 5. COMMAND-LINE FORMAT
General shape:
  --nstates N  --s<I> B [behaviour params] --cle<I> L  --n<I> T  [--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>]
<I> is the state index, <K> the index of a transition leaving that state. Both start at 0.

## A. Global
* `--nstates N` : number of states, integer in [1..4].

## B. Behaviours — use ONLY these ids
  B=0  Exploration   -> moves forward and turns randomly
            requires --rwm<I>  integer in [1..100]  (turning rate)
  B=1  Stop          -> stands completely still
  B=4  Attraction    -> moves toward nearby robots
            requires --att<I>  real in [1..5]  (attraction gain)
  B=5  Repulsion     -> moves away from nearby robots
            requires --rep<I>  real in [1..5]  (repulsion gain)
  B=6  Spiral        -> traces an expanding spiral
            requires --spd<I>  integer in [1..100]  (spiral speed)
  B=8  GoToColor     -> moves toward the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=9  GoAwayColor   -> moves away from the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=10  FlockCenter   -> moves toward the centre of the group
            requires --fc<I>  integer in [1..100]  (flocking gain)
  B=11  ZigZag        -> moves in a zigzag pattern
            requires --zfr<I>  integer in [1..100]  (zigzag frequency)

Every state also takes an LED colour:
* `--cle<I> L` : one of {0,4,5,6}  (0=off, 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)

## C. Transitions
* `--n<I> T` : number of transitions leaving state <I>, integer in [1..4].
  Up to 4 transitions may leave the same state, each with a different condition type.
* `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
  With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
  With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.
* `--c<I>x<K> C` : condition type — use ONLY these ids
  C=0  floor under the robot is BLACK
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=1  floor under the robot is GRAY
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=2  floor under the robot is WHITE
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=3  number of neighbours ABOVE a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=4  number of neighbours BELOW a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=5  fires with a fixed probability at every step
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=7  the robot PERCEIVES a given colour with its camera
            requires --l<I>x<K>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (colour to look for)
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])

# 6. HARD RULES
1. Integers stay integers; real-valued parameters always carry a decimal point (1.0, not 1).
2. Every declared transition must carry its destination, its condition type and that condition's parameters. A missing parameter crashes the simulator.
3. `--p<I>x<K>` is a PROBABILITY for conditions 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, but an INTEGER COUNT OF NEIGHBOURS for conditions 3 and 4. Never write 0.8 where a neighbour count is expected.

# 7. YOU ARE THE EVOLUTIONARY OPERATOR
This is a multi-round conversation on one real ARGoS3 optimisation run. The numerical optimiser is restarted from scratch at the beginning of every round, so exploration and exploitation are entirely up to you.

1. SURVIVAL (elitism): copy the current best-ever machine verbatim in your answer so its lineage is not lost.
2. EXPLOITATION: spend about half of your machines on small edits of the best performers — shift a probability, a threshold or a gain by roughly ten percent, and keep the topology.
3. EXPLORATION: spend the rest on genuinely different topologies or module combinations, to escape local optima.
4. RICHNESS: do not let every state settle on a single transition of the same condition type. Include candidates with two to four transitions per state mixing different condition types.
